Let PN (W, ζ) be the exact nonlinear normal-fiber chart proved in Sections 2.4– 3.2, and let Jcoarea be its
exact finite-volume coarea density. On the inner regular chart, write dγj (ζ) for the normalized Gaussian
with covariance Cjd .
Theorem 4.13 (Exact regular-reference identity). At finite volume and before any thermodynamic or
cutoff limit, the inner regular part of one Wilson fiber integral has the exact form

not a Gaussian approximation. All nonquadratic Wilson terms, the complete field-dependent Haar and

visible in Vj or in the charged irregular operators. No Gaussian product factorization across spatial
components is asserted.
Proof. Apply the exact finite-volume normal-fiber and coarea identity to the compact Wilson integral in

displayed formula then follows by the definition of Vj and the once-only normalization cj . The exact
partition into inner, curvature, collar, and outer labels is made before logarithms and is recombined
before reflection positivity is used. Gauge covariance, signed-hypercubic covariance, and aligned-reflection
compatibility follow from covariance of the Karcher map, its normal section, the tree gauge, and the
scalar Feynman precision. Individual artificial Gaussian detail measures are not claimed to be reflection
positive.

is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,

without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated

Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,

   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

     by Cs,A,B e−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied
     by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is
     claimed.
The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family.
Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose
dloc so the variable x = mw (d − CR )+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with
rate at least mw /2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 12 min{mtr , msp }. Hence one may
take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not
degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put

and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.
Theorem 8.9 (Uniform physical two-source connector). There are dloc , A0 , m > 0, independent of
resolution, microscopic cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions, with the following properties.
 (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR (z) and BR (z ′ ), the complete
     connected majorant is bounded at every separation by
                                                                                       ′
                            Cm (BR (z), BR (z ′ )) ≤ Km (z, z ′ ) := A0 e−m(|z−z |1 −2R)+ ,            (117)

     and                                 X                             X
                               sup ℓ4m        Km (z, z ′ ) + sup ℓ4m       Km (z, z ′ ) ≤ K0 .         (118)
                                z                              z′
                                         z′                            z

 (b) If either buffer carries a fixed finite renormalized local source, displacement mark, or rotation mark,
     then for test sources with disjoint supports at distance d ≥ dloc the connected functional is bounded
     by Cs,A,B e−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied
     by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is
     claimed.
The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family.
Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose
dloc so the variable x = mw (d − CR )+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with
rate at least mw /2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 12 min{mtr , msp }. Hence one may
take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not
degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

allocation to a spanning tree on {1, . . . , n}. An edge joining bounded positive-scale roots is bounded by

for one finite Schwartz seminorm pN depending on the source types. If the source labels split into two

Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into

summation of the remaining edges, with one Schwartz L1 norm at the tree root, gives (120) and the
separated-group exponential.
Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-
mations are changes of variables or observable identities, not replacement probability measures, and all
regular/irregular and half-cylinder labels are recombined before the OS form is taken. Therefore reflection
positivity passes to the common limiting family.
Proof. At finite cutoff and aligned reflection volume, let Fi be any finite family of constructed positive-time
observables. Pull each Fi back through the exact block, fiber, and source identities to a gauge-invariant
function of the original positive-time links. Exact recombination makes this pullback independent of the
auxiliary labels. Reflection positivity of the Wilson measure gives
                                           X

                                           i,j

The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization
factors remain inside the same normalized integral once. Every entry of the finite matrix converges along
the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This
proves the claim.
Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.

All constants are uniform in cutoff, original volume and finite directed depth.
Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the
scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,
determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories
and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle;
its reported hull has length at least cMi . Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives
Ce−σ0 Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0 /u .
   For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent
one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift
pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0 Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported
hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way.


                                                       97
Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the
rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification.
   At R = R⋆ (u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum
coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an
unpaired periodic image may already be of order εi u. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable
cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a
prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the
charged flat row and εi+1 . The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves
(194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the
scalar comparison.
   Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the
distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector,
or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the
statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor,
while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks.

Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric

===== \bC0\b HIT 5 @ 85986 =====
canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.

a fixed polynomial in u−1 , while the denominator is the trial-ball lower bound [c0 ud0 /2 e−CRg ]|X| ; the

variables are integrated. Cross-core words remain in the explicit connected row. Summing (HT3) proves
the collar/irregular score row.
   Higher pivot derivatives are obtained by differentiating the normalized-score identity. They are finite
Bell polynomials in derivatives of the complete local action, Jacobian, and normalized cumulants. The
fixed-group atlas and the C ∞ splice templates supply these derivatives through order 2sG ; the plateau
denominator and differentiated total-variation bounds control each normalized cumulant without a
core-volume factor. Thus the hth derivative costs only a fixed polynomial in u−1 .
   Because u ranges in the fixed compact interval I⋆ , all remaining finite powers of u−1 are absorbed into
the displayed CG,h (1 + u−h ) hierarchy. A finite atlas covers the one-pivot compact fiber, so the score
constants are uniform in U , exterior data, volume, and preceding weak depth. Integrating the first score
along a geodesic of length at most diam G proves the oscillation bound. Marking one fixed factor uses
the corresponding marked rows.
   For a finite family of active straight macro-paths, keep every fine link on those active paths in its
original link coordinate. An active path carries one nontrivial retained Peter–Weyl coefficient, or one
bounded scalar probe whose first derivative at the origin is centered. Choose live pivots P = {p1 , . . . , pN }
among the original links of the active paths, with mutually disjoint buffered neighborhoods separated
by distance R and separated from all path endpoints. Condition on every nonpivot fine link, every link
outside the active paths, and all exterior/source data; denote this collection by η. Do not condition on a
retained path product carrying an active coefficient or probe.
   Assign every physical factor by its incidence in the original fine-link support: factors meeting no live
pivot enter H0 , complete owned factors meeting exactly pi enter the ith one-pivot weight, and connected
factors meeting at least two live pivots enter the multipivot remainder. The active retained carriers are
distinguished insertions and are never absorbed into a one-pivot density or counted as physical activities.

Lemma 6.32 (Cutoff-uniform junction row). Every fixed bulk, toron, seam, reflection, or wrapping
junction has operator norm at most one constant J∗ < ∞, independent of the Peter–Weyl cutoff. At
every fixed passive mark order k ≤ 3 + Q, the corresponding coefficientwise constants J∗,k are finite and

Proof. In block-first Hilbert normalization, Clebsch–Gordan recouplings are unitary, invariant-channel
maps are orthogonal projections, and translations and inversion are unitary. All remaining maps belong
to a finite bounded-incidence template set. Passive marks differentiate only the fixed local coefficient
maps and are controlled by the fixed-Q marked row.

Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner

each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.

                                                     74
Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno

===== \bC0\b HIT 16 @ 319746 =====
tion and irregular rows, multiplied by the
fixed tree, incidence, and covariance-bond constants, is below one. Choose jw so that uj ≤ uw for every
j ≥ jw . The Banach-valued one/two-root estimate leaves the source buffers outside the nonroot activity
row; hence it changes only the root constant and not σ. Spatial decay is carried by the exponential bond
on the distinguished root-to-root tree path, rather than by an assertion that the conditional covariance
has strict finite range.
Lemma 8.4 (Weak-scale connector). Fix two physical source buffers A, B and let d = dist(A, B). The
sum of connected scale-j histories whose coarsest nonlocal object occurs at a weak scale j ≥ jw is bounded,
after Riemann source normalization, by

                               C exp{− min{c0 | log σ|, mopt }(d − CR )+ /ℓj }.                        (113)

The corresponding source kernel has uniformly bounded Riemann-weighted row and column sums.
Proof. Absorb every family contained in one source buffer into its exact local conditional. A remaining
connected family joining the buffers has a support tree with a distinguished path from A to B. Assign
each Gaussian contraction the bond
                                           Ke−mopt d(X,Y )/ℓj
of Corollary 4.14, and report every fixed local connector word in the adjacent support. Along the
distinguished path the triangle inequality gives
                                 Y
                                         e−mopt d(X,Y )/ℓj ≤ e−mopt (d−CR )+ /ℓj .
                             (X,Y ) on path

All vertex constants and the factors K are summed by the strict rooted margin of Lemma 8.3; unused
branches are summed by the remaining KP reserve. Weakening the smaller of the covariance exponent
and the rooted support exponent once gives (113), with c0 | log σ| there replaced, if necessary, by a smaller
fixed positive constant. Passive source buffers are controlled by the exact full-fine two-buffer theorem,
Corollary 7.4. Pulling the connector back to the fine source grid does not enlarge its Schur row or column
norm because of Lemma 8.1.

Lemma 8.5 (Matching-scale spacetime connector). For bounded matching-scale cylinders F, G supported
in physical sets A, B,
                           |Cov(F, G)| ≤ CF,G exp{−m0 dist1 (A, B)}                           (114)
with m0 > 0 independent of spatial volume and microscopic cutoff.
Proof. Use F and G as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The matching forest is four-dimensional:
its distinguished root path pays the same optical, Haar-carrier, and reported-support exponents in
spatial and Euclidean-time directions. The weighted row and column estimate therefore gives (114) with
m0 = mE ; fixed endpoint layers are absorbed into CF,G .


                                                     74
Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursive

===== \bC0\b HIT 17 @ 325936 =====
e disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1
                                                         w .



                                                     75
For d ≥ dw ,                   X
                                      Ce−c0 | log σ|(d−CR )/ℓj ≤ C ′ e−mw (d−CR )/2 .                  (116)
                               j≥jw

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put
x = mw (d − CR )+ . The scale sum is bounded by C r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1,
and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.
Theorem 8.9 (Uniform physical two-source connector). There are dloc , A0 , m > 0, independent of
resolution, microscopic cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions, with the following properties.
 (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR (z) and BR (z ′ ), the complete
     connected majorant is bounded at every separation by
                                                                                       ′
                            Cm (BR (z), BR (z ′ )) ≤ Km (z, z ′ ) := A0 e−m(|z−z |1 −2R)+ ,            (117)

     and                                 X                             X
                               sup ℓ4m        Km (z, z ′ ) + sup ℓ4m       Km (z, z ′ ) ≤ K0 .         (118)
                                z                              z′
                                         z′                            z

 (b) If either buffer carries a fixed finite renormalized local source, displacement mark, or rotation mark,
     then for test sources with disjoint supports at distance d ≥ dloc the connected functional is bounded
     by Cs,A,B e−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied
     by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is
     claimed.
The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family.
Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose
dloc so the variable x = mw (d − CR )+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with
rate at least mw /2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 12 min{mtr , msp }. Hence one may
take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not
degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the
scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,
determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories
and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle;
its reported hull has length at least cMi . Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives
Ce−σ0 Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0 /u .
   For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent
one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift
pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0 Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported
hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way.

Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the
rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification.
   At R = R⋆ (u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum
coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an
unpaired periodic image may already be of order εi u. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable
cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a
prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the
charged flat row and εi+1 . The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves
(194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the
scalar comparison.

scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,
determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories
and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle;
its reported hull has length at least cMi . Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives
Ce−σ0 Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0 /u .
   For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent
one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift
pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0 Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported
hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way.


                                                       97
Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the
rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification.
   At R = R⋆ (u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum
coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an
unpaired periodic image may already be of order εi u. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable
cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a
prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the
charged flat row and εi+1 . The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves
(194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the
scalar comparison.
   Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the
distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector,
or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the
statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor,
while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks.

The lower bound is independent of M , microscopic spacing, volume, and boundary condition. Passing to
M → ∞ cannot decrease the exponent.

Proof. The first-crossing value, the weak threshold, and the finite macrobridge parameters are fixed
in physical units before the microscopic depth is sent to infinity. Hence σ, mopt , mE , ℓjw , and ℓ0 are
independent of M . For finer weak scales, ℓ−1      −1
                                            j ≥ ℓjw , so the weak rate is minimized at the coarsest weak
scale. The finitely many intermediate scales alter only the prefactor, while the joint Haar–optical theorem
supplies the same matching exponent mE at every cutoff. Taking the minimum gives exactly m∗ in
(111).

such that, for every j ≥ jw , the exact source-free scale-j interaction and irregular remainder satisfy
                           X

After one or two fixed source buffers are retained, every additional nonroot vertex in the rooted connected

Proof. The Gate–G1 invariant graph gives a uniform support-summed interaction bound ∥Vj ∥a ≤ Cuj
                                                                                                         −α
on the weak corridor, while the regular/irregular split gives a rooted remainder bounded by Cu−pj e
                                                                                                    −cuj

Corollary 4.14 gives a volume- and depth-uniform Schur row for every Gaussian bond. Since uj → 0
toward the ultraviolet, choose uw so that the sum of the interaction and irregular rows, multiplied by the
fixed tree, incidence, and covariance-bond constants, is below one. Choose jw so that uj ≤ uw for every
j ≥ jw . The Banach-valued one/two-root estimate leaves the source buffers outside the nonroot activity
row; hence it changes only the root constant and not σ. Spatial decay is carried by the exponential bond
on the distinguished root-to-root tree path, rather than by an assertion that the conditional covariance
has strict finite range.
Lemma 8.4 (Weak-scale connector). Fix two physical source buffers A, B and let d = dist(A, B). The
sum of connected scale-j histories whose coarsest nonlocal object occurs at a weak scale j ≥ jw is bounded,
after Riemann source normalization, by

                               C exp{− min{c0 | log σ|, mopt }(d − CR )+ /ℓj }.                        (113)

The corresponding source kernel has uniformly bounded Riemann-weighted row and column sums.
Proof. Absorb every family contained in one source buffer into its exact local conditional. A remaining
connected family joining the buffers has a support tree with a distinguished path from A to B. Assign
each Gaussian contraction the bond
                                           Ke−mopt d(X,Y )/ℓj
of Corollary 4.14, and report every fixed local connector word in the adjacent support. Along the
distinguished path the triangle inequality gives
                                 Y
                                         e−mopt d(X,Y )/ℓj ≤ e−mopt (d−CR )+ /ℓj .
                             (X,Y ) on path

All vertex constants and the factors K are summed by the strict rooted margin of Lemma 8.3; unused
branches are summed by the remaining KP reserve. Weakening the smaller of the covariance exponent
and the rooted support exponent once gives (113), with c0 | log σ| there replaced, if necessary, by a smaller
fixed positive constant. Passive source buffers are controlled by the exact full-fine two-buffer theorem,
Corollary 7.4. Pulling the connector back to the fine source grid does not enlarge its Schur row or column
norm because of Lemma 8.1.

Lemma 8.5 (Matching-scale spacetime connector). For bounded matching-scale cylinders F, G supported
in physical sets A, B,
                           |Cov(F, G)| ≤ CF,G exp{−m0 dist1 (A, B)}                           (114)
with m0 > 0 independent of spatial volume and microscopic cutoff.
Proof. Use F and G as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The matching forest is four-dimensional:
its distinguished root path pays the same optical, Haar-carrier, and reported-support exponents in
spatial and Euclidean-time directions. The weighted row and column estimate therefore gives (114) with
m0 = mE ; fixed endpoint layers are absorbed into CF,G .

coarea logarithm is the finite-volume analytic matrix logarithm of I + DN P , whose inverse is bounded
by (1 − η)−1 .
   The localization projector has a finite template norm polynomial in the support radius. The reported
shortest connector paths and finite collars preserve exponent κ. Canonical hull ownership assigns each
history once.

where Bm is the finite multivariable Bell polynomial supplied by the chain rule through order m.
Proof. Compose the six bounded maps. Through order three, the chain rule contains only finitely

and the derived output allocation is at most b∗ . This is an invariant ball inequality, not circular use of
the output theorem as an input.

4.7    Macrostep contraction
Let ΦL be the exact L = 2s macrostep map after vacuum, Yang–Mills, and declared local-jet extraction.
Split its stable derivative as
                                      DΦL |stable = JL + NL ,
where JL is the finite local jet block and NL contains all reported nonlocal rows.
Theorem 4.30 (Local/nonlocal separation). Assume:
1. the one-step rooted marked supplier bounds are finite:
                                          Km < ∞,             0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
   uniformly under the standin

===== δB HIT 4 @ 297862 =====
                       Z(J)                                X       Y
                                        =           X,J (ζX )
                                                  dµref                             zA,J (ζA ).          (100)
                               Zloc (J)
                                              X                 F compatible A∈F

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .




                                                          69
Lemma 6.41 (Admission to the joint rooted ball). Let δB > 0 be the strict admission threshold in
Theorem 3.31 and the summed replica–BKAR theorem, and put

                       mbase (u, Rinv , RG ) := C0 η0 (u, Rinv ) + θu + κG (RG ) .         (101)
                                                                                

Choose the compact first-crossing interval and Rinv , then enlarge RG , so that
                                                         1                           1
                           sup mbase (u, Rinv , RG ) ≤     δB ,      CE ηH (RG ) ≤     δB .                 (102)
                          u∈I⋆                           2                           4

Then the complete joint branch mass is at most 3δB /4, strictly inside the same admission ball.
Proof. The regular supplier gives η0 ≤ C(uω + ∥R⋆ (u)∥[3] ), the bad-core mass is exponentially small, and
both κG (RG ) and ηH (RG ) tend to zero as RG → ∞. Choose the parameters in the displayed order. This
is the same strict inequality consumed by the existing irregular and Gaussian forest theorems.
Lemma 6.42 (Cycle-safe compact spatial row). The complete compact activity estimate is exactly (99).
In particular every discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch is weighted before summation,
and ηH (RG ) ≤ Ce−mH RG for one mH > 0.
Proof. This is the final paragraph of Section 6.5.4; the residual edge-weight reserve pays root and anchor
multiplicity.
6.5.5 One mixed Haar–optical connected forest
The regular, irregular, and Gaussian cross-core rows are collected in mbase , while the complete nonvacuum
compact row is ηH . Lemma 6.41 supplies their common strict admission margin. Thus
                                                                             3
                            E := mbase (u, Rinv , RG ) + CE ηH (RG ) ≤
                           mbr                                                 δB < δB .                    (103)
                                                                             4
Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

prescribed f

retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .
Theorem 6.44 (Two composite-root extension). Fix two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B. For any
prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into
                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale
roots, or normalized local roots measured in the permanent finite source-renormalization norm. Then
                                        (Q )   (Q )
                                  K(RA A , RB B ) ≤ CQA ,QB e−mE dist(A,B) ,                                (105)

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source
orders and root types, but not on volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, or preceding weak depth.


                                                         70
Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert
those two coefficients as the two distinguished roots of the exact joint compact–optical logarithm; do
not represent their internal source leaves as additional bulk source marks. Every pivot and connector
lying wholly inside either fixed root buffer is absorbed into its root constant. Outside the two buffers the
nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict
branch coefficient are exactly those of Theorem 6.43. The unique connecting path therefore pays the
same spatial exponent mE . For normalized local fields the weak triangular source theorem supplies the
finite root seminorm before this matching-scale insertion. Thus arbitrary finite local source order changes
only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

common live-pivot product tube.
   For the marked numerator, every factor outside this analytic inventory is a fixed-template real factor.
Its enlarged-cell support has uniformly bounded diameter, so the enlarged matching separation makes it
incident to at most one pivot buffer. This proves the disjoint assignment to the sets Rp and R∅ .
   We first verify the ordered-word identity. For one pivot and one word, differentiate the sequential flow
(220) from the last variable inward. Induction gives

                         ∂tp,1 · · · ∂tp,rp c(Φp,tp (Up ))           = Dp,ap,1 · · · Dp,ap,rp c(Up ).               (225)
                                                             tp =0

The same argument with the unused variables set to zero identifies every subset coefficient with the
corresponding ordered subword. Formula (222) therefore has exactly the same square-free mixed coefficients
as the actual smooth factor. Products of these jets reproduce all Leibniz coefficients in which every word
variable is used at most once. That is precisely the coefficient required after assigning a distinct variable
to every letter of every Sobolev word. Analytic factors follow the actual sequential flow. Since variables
attached to distinct pivots act on distinct group copies, the full mixed coefficient agrees with the product
of the prescribed ordered words. The hybrid numerator is only an auxiliary holomorphic interpolation
away from the origin; no regularity is promoted.
   Let ηref < δBKAR be the rooted residual-species mass of the undeformed one-core reference at the
compact crossing interval, and put εBKAR := δBKAR − ηref > 0. The strict reserve is part of Lemma 4.27
after the fixed crossing and bad-action parameters have been selected. Let λres > 0 be the unused part
of the printed pivot-incidence exponent after the old nuclear and coverage costs. The total complex
displacement at one pivot is at most CG rp ∥tp ∥∞ ≤ 2CG sG ∥tp ∥∞ . Thus, on ∥t∥∞ ≤ τ , analytic multipivot

incidence reserve. Because the pivot buffers are disjoint and the rooted norm is a parent-normalized
supremum, the total additional reference child mass is bounded by
                                                                       2sG

not by C|K|τ . Choose one τhyb so that the right side is less than εBKAR /2.
   The proof of Lemma 4.27 now applies coefficientwise for every complex vector in this ordered product
polydisc. The Gram bound is unchanged, and the exact Pruefer identity sums the tree degrees with one
parent-normalized child sum strictly below one. Hence the connected log series converges absolutely and
locally uniformly. At finite volume it is the logarithm of the exact deformed integral near the origin;
analytic continuation by the same convergent series gives GX (t) = GX (0)eLX (t) throughout the connected
polydisc, which proves zero-freeness without a positivity claim at complex t.
    Only clusters intersecting one of the K pivot buffers contribute to LX (t) − LX (0). Root the first such
intersection at the least pivot buffer. The number rp ≤ 2sG of variables inside that buffer is a fixed
group-dependent constant, so the same parent-normalized tree sum is bounded uniformly per buffer.

    For the distinguished marked numerator, use the one-root version of the same absolutely convergent
tree expansion. Each fixed pivot buffer contains only a bounded number of real finite-template factors.
Their ordered multiaffine jets have at most 22sG coefficients apiece, and the charge ledger bounds all
coefficients through order 2sG . Since G is fixed, the jet product on the closed half-polydisc costs at
most eCG |K| . The fixed optical and physical source derivatives are inserted as the already admitted
finite marked species and change only Cm,Q . The pre-owner physical mark supplies only the fixed score
list of (218) and the trajectory factor ΛN,m . An ordered real pivot jet hitting a cutoff is supported on
the charged event of Lemma 3.5; recollecting the unique set of differentiated cutoff occurrences before
ownership gives a disjoint exhaustive augmented component family. The component action charge and
plateau denominator row supply bu (X), and the tree sum supplies only eChyb |K| . This proves (224) and
the all-in clause.

Lemma 4.28 (Finite uniform module constants). On the common weak tube,
                             chart
                            Km        coa
                                   , Km      loc
                                          , Km      hull
                                                 , Km       rec
                                                         , Km   < ∞,      0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
with constants uniform under the standing convention.
Proof. The finite-volume normal fiber equation has a uniform contraction parameter η < 1; its derivatives
are finite products of the stable resolvent, P , D2 N , and D3 N in the exponential matrix algebra. The
coarea logarithm is the finite-volume analytic matrix logarithm of I + DN P , whose inverse is bounded
by (1 − η)−1 .
   The localization projector has a finite template norm polynomial in the support radius. The reported
shortest connector paths and finite collars preserve exponent κ. Canonical hull ownership assigns each
history once.
   For a post-localization output RP , put
                             gP = ∥DRP (0)∥,           hP = sup ∥D2 RP (η + itθ)∥.

Theorem 4.29 (Permanent one-step envelope). For every prescribed finite source order N , there are
finite constants
                                 Km < ∞,       0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
independent of volume, microscopic cutoff, and preceding weak depth, such that the exact regular one-step
map satisfies
                                         ∥Dm Φreg ∥κ;3,N ≤ Km
on one reproduced weak ball.
   More explicitly,
                                 chart    coa    BKAR    loc    hull    rec
                                                                            
                        Km ≤ Bm K≤m    , K≤m  , K≤m   , K≤m  , K≤m   , K≤m    ,
where Bm is the finite multivariable Bell polynomial supplied by the chain rule through order m.
Proof. Compose the six bounded maps. Through order three, the chain rule contains only finitely
many partitions. Every term is bounded in the same permanent κ-weighted norm and carries the same
active/source inventory.
   The input allocation cone is an assumption of the weak activity ball; the output cone is derived after
the exact connected output by recertification. Choose the ball so
                                                  C(uω + r) ≤ b∗
and the derived output allocation is at most b∗ . This is an invariant ball inequality, not circular use of
the output theorem as an input.

                                          Km < ∞,             0 ≤ m ≤ 3,

   For the marked numerator, every factor outside this analytic inventory is a fixed-template real factor.
Its enlarged-cell support has uniformly bounded diameter, so the enlarged matching separation makes it
incident to at most one pivot buffer. This proves the disjoint assignment to the sets Rp and R∅ .
   We first verify the ordered-word identity. For one pivot and one word, differentiate the sequential flow
(220) from the last variable inward. Induction gives

                         ∂tp,1 · · · ∂tp,rp c(Φp,tp (Up ))           = Dp,ap,1 · · · Dp,ap,rp c(Up ).               (225)

The same argument with the unused variables set to zero identifies every subset coefficient with the
corresponding ordered subword. Formula (222) therefore has exactly the same square-free mixed coefficients
as the actual smooth factor. Products of these jets reproduce all Leibniz coefficients in which every word
variable is used at most once. That is precisely the coefficient required after assigning a distinct variable
to every letter of every Sobolev word. Analytic factors follow the actual sequential flow. Since variables
attached to distinct pivots act on distinct group copies, the full mixed coefficient agrees with the product
of the prescribed ordered words. The hybrid numerator is only an auxiliary holomorphic interpolation
away from the origin; no regularity is promoted.
   Let ηref < δBKAR be the rooted residual-species mass of the undeformed one-core reference at the

after the fixed crossing and bad-action parameters have been selected. Let λres > 0 be the unused part
of the printed pivot-incidence exponent after the old nuclear and coverage costs. The total complex
displacement at one pivot is at most CG rp ∥tp ∥∞ ≤ 2CG sG ∥tp ∥∞ . Thus, on ∥t∥∞ ≤ τ , analytic multipivot

not by C|K|τ . Choose one τhyb so that the right side is less than εBKAR /2.
   The proof of Lemma 4.27 now applies coefficientwise for every complex vector in this ordered product
polydisc. The Gram bound is unchanged, and the exact Pruefer identity sums the tree degrees with one
parent-normalized child sum strictly below one. Hence the connected log series converges absolutely and
locally uniformly. At finite volume it is the logarithm of the exact deformed integral near the origin;
analytic continuation by the same convergent series gives GX (t) = GX (0)eLX (t) throughout the connected
polydisc, which proves zero-freeness without a positivity claim at complex t.
    Only clusters intersecting one of the K pivot buffers contribute to LX (t) − LX (0). Root the first such
intersection at the least pivot buffer. The number rp ≤ 2sG of variables inside that buffer is a fixed
group-dependent constant, so the same parent-normalized tree sum is bounded uniformly per buffer.
Every cluster is assigned once; summing the |K| possible roots gives |LX (t)| ≤ Chyb |K|.
    For the distinguished marked numerator, use the one-root version of the same absolutely convergent
tree expansion. Each fixed pivot buffer contains only a bounded number of real finite-template factors.
Their ordered multiaffine jets have at most 22sG coefficients apiece, and the charge ledger bounds all
coefficients through order 2sG . Since G is fixed, the jet product on the closed half-polydisc costs at
most eCG |K| . The fixed optical and physical source derivatives are inserted as the already admitted
finite marked species and change only Cm,Q . The pre-owner physical mark supplies only the fixed score
list of (218) and the trajectory factor ΛN,m . An ordered real pivot jet hitting a cutoff is supported on
the charged event of Lemma 3.5; recollecting the unique set of differentiated cutoff occurrences before
ownership gives a disjoint exhaustive augmented component family. The component action charge and
plateau denominator row supply bu (X), and the tree sum supplies only eChyb |K| . This proves (224) and
the all-in clause.

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Lemma 6.41 (Admission to the joint rooted ball). Let δB > 0 be the strict admission threshold in
Theorem 3.31 and the summed replica–BKAR theorem, and put

                       mbase (u, Rinv , RG ) := C0 η0 (u, Rinv ) + θu + κG (RG ) .         (101)

Choose the compact first-crossing interval and Rinv , then enlarge RG , so that
                                                         1                           1
                           sup mbase (u, Rinv , RG ) ≤     δB ,      CE ηH (RG ) ≤     δB .                 (102)
                          u∈I⋆                           2                           4

Then the complete joint branch mass is at most 3δB /4, strictly inside the same admission ball.
Proof. The regular supplier gives η0 ≤ C(uω + ∥R⋆ (u)∥[3] ), the bad-core mass is exponentially small, and
both κG (RG ) and ηH (RG ) tend to zero as RG → ∞. Choose the parameters in the displayed order. This
is the same strict inequality consumed by the existing irregular and Gaussian forest theorems.
Lemma 6.42 (Cycle-safe compact spatial row). The complete compact activity estimate is exactly (99).
In particular every discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch is weighted before summation,
and ηH (RG ) ≤ Ce−mH RG for one mH > 0.
Proof. This is the final paragraph of Section 6.5.4; the residual edge-weight reserve pays root and anchor
multiplicity.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
